         | `*/seeders.ts`                                               |\r\n| Schemas                   | `*/schemas.ts`                                               |\r\n| Compiled contracts        | `*/contracts.ts`                                             |\r\n| Relations                 | `*/relations.ts`                                             |\r\n| Error classes/guards      | `*/errors.ts`                                                |\r\n| Public exports            | `*/index.ts`                                                 |\r\n| Implementations           | `*/[domain]/[Entity].ts` — one class per file                |\r\n| Function modules          | a designated folder's `[function].ts`, one function per file |\r\n\r\nUse only the centralized files an environment needs.\r\n\r\n## Declaration placement\r\n\r\n- An implementation file contains imports and exactly one class implementation with `#` fields.\r\n- It contains no module-scope interface, type, constant, or free function—even when private to that file.\r\n- A function module contains imports and exactly one exported function named for its file. Everything else extracts by kind exactly as it does from a class module.\r\n- Extract local declarations by kind. “Only used here” and “not exported” are not exemptions.\r\n- Every declaration in a centralized file is exported. Fold away a trivial single-use declaration or export/test it; never leave it hidden.\r\n- The only permitted non-exported module-scope declarations are in a runtime entrypoint that must be self-contained and cannot import siblings, such as raw source loaded in a worker. Explain that necessity in a comment.\r\n- A runtime entry—`src/bin/main.ts`, `app/browser/main.ts`, `app/server/main.ts`—is a fixed name, not a centralized kind file. Both the data rule and the function rule reach it, so it declares no module-scope constant and no module-scope function: it imports what it needs and runs. The preceding self-contained exception covers only an entrypoint that cannot import siblings.\r\n- Perform a cleanup pass after implementation: no stray implementation-file declarations, non-exported/wrong-kind centralized declarations, prohibited nested declarations, duplicate implementations, compatibility aliases, superfluous wrappers, stale imports/barrel rows, or untested extracted functions.\r\n\r\n## Kind purity\r\n\r\n- Each centralized file contains only its named kind.\r\n- Module-scope constants live only in `constants.ts`, use UPPER_SNAKE_CASE, and freeze object/array data with `Object.freeze`.\r\n- A camelCase namespace containing functions is helper behavior, not constant data; place it in `helpers.ts`.\r\n- `helpers.ts` is exported reusable infrastructure. A reusable regex, parser, header flattener, signer, or similar fragment has one implementation used by source, tests, and fixtures.\r\n- A would-be helper has these outcomes:\r\n  - trivial and genuinely single-use → fold into its caller;\r\n  - non-trivial or reusable → extract, export, unit-test, and route every duplicate through it.\r\n- `factories.ts`, `compilers.ts`, and `parsers.ts` are centralized files, not hiding places. Factory glue extracts to `helpers.ts`; pure compiler/parser recursion remains exported in its own kind file.\r\n- Every exported function in `parsers.ts` is named `parse*`. Every exported function in\r\n  `factories.ts` is named `create*`.\r\n- Those name forms are one-directional. A name does not place a function: `createWriteDirectory`\r\n  creates a directory rather than an entity and `isVacant` is a predicate rather than a `Guard<T>`,\r\n  so both stay in `helpers.ts`. Placement follows what the function is; the name form follows\r\n  placement.\r\n- Repair a violation of those forms by deciding what the function **is** first, then moving or\r\n  renaming to match. Both repairs exist and picking the wrong one does real damage.\r\n  - Wrong file, right name → **move it**. A `scan*` in `parsers.ts` is a pure lexical leaf that\r\n    belongs in `helpers.ts`. The barrel star-exports both, so the move leaves the published surface\r\n    identical.\r\n  - Right file, wrong name → **rename it in place**. An entity is a class instance whose methods\r\n    drive its own state, as opposed to a plain value that carries data and no behaviour. A function\r\n    returning a live entity is an entity factory and belongs in `factories.ts` whatever it is\r\n    called, so `restoreThing` there is misnamed, not misplaced. Renaming moves the published\r\n    surface and earns a version bump; that cost is the correct one to pay, and it is smaller than\r\n    the alternative.\r\n  - Never let the name choose. Relocating a correctly-placed function to escape a rename drags its\r\n    dependencies with it — an entity factory moved into `helpers.ts` makes that file import an\r\n    implementation class, and a leaf file that imports a class stops being a leaf for every module\r\n    beneath it.\r\n- Keep the leaf pair class-free. `helpers.ts` and `validators.ts` sit at the bottom of a module's\r\n  graph: they import types, constants, errors, and each other, and they import no implementation\r\n  class. Every file that constructs or drives a class — `cloners.ts`, `compilers.ts`, `factories.ts`,\r\n  `shapers.ts` — sits above them, consumes them, and is never consumed by them. One cycle between\r\n  the leaves is the shape this produces and is acceptable; an edge running downward from a\r\n  class-importing file into the leaf pair is not.\r\n- `templates.ts` and `contracts.ts` hold data only — shipped template definitions and compiled\r\n  contracts. A function that builds either belongs in the kind file for what it builds.\r\n- `handlers.ts` holds request handlers, which are functions. `routes.ts` holds data only: a route is\r\n  `{ method, path, handler }`, so the table maps each path to a handler declared in `handlers.ts`\r\n  and referenced by name, never to a function expression written in place. Neither file mixes kinds and the\r\n  route set stays readable as data.\r\n\r\n### What the policy instruments prove\r\n\r\nThe `policy` Oxlint plugin (`configs/policy.ts`) enforces syntactic placement: a declaration of a\r\ngiven syntactic kind appears only in a file permitted to hold that kind. The fleet policy sweep\r\n(`tests/policy.test.ts`, `tests/setupPolicy.ts`) enforces what a path or a text reading decides.\r\nThe plugin reads declaration syntax and file name; the sweep reads paths and workspace text;\r\nneither reads meaning.\r\n\r\n- The plugin proves that a module function sits in a function-kind file, that module data sits in a\r\n  data-kind file, that every centralized declaration is exported, that a class sits in its matching\r\n  implementation or errors file, and that `constants.ts` declares only UPPER_SNAKE_CASE consts with\r\n  no bare collection literal.\r\n- The sweep proves that no source, test, config, or script file carries an `eslint-disable` or\r\n  `oxlint-disable` directive.\r\n- The sweep proves that every `.claude/rules/*.md` file has a rule-map row in `AGENTS.md` and that\r\n  every row resolves to a file.\r\n- The sweep proves the host portability rules that are path- or text-shaped over the population\r\n  `POLICY_PORTABILITY_GLOB` names: no path segment carries a Windows reserved device name, a\r\n  character Windows refuses, a trailing dot or space, or a sibling differing only by case; and no\r\n  `package.json` script names a `.sh` file.\r\n- The plugin proves, over the population `POLICY_ENDING_GLOBS` names, that no source trims a\r\n  payload before splitting it on `'\\n'`, reads `os.EOL`, or imports `EOL` from `node:os`.\r\n- The sweep cannot write a filename the host refuses. A Windows host rejects `<`, folds a case\r\n  collision into one file, and turns `:` into an alternate data stream, so those boundaries are\r\n  proven from a path population rather than from written files.\r\n- The plugin does not prove a collection is frozen. It reads the declaration, never the value a\r\n  call returns, so `Object.freeze([…])` and any other call initializer are one syntax to it. The\r\n  freeze obligation in the earlier kind-purity rules binds regardless; only the bare literal is\r\n  mechanical.\r\n- The plugin does not tell one function kind from another. Every centralized file that permits\r\n  functions reads the same to it apart from the `parse*` and `create*` name forms: `cloners.ts`,\r\n  `combinators.ts`, `compilers.ts`, `errors.ts`, `factories.ts`, `handlers.ts`, `helpers.ts`,\r\n  `inferers.ts`, `middlewares.ts`, `parsers.ts`, `relations.ts`, `schemas.ts`, `seeders.ts`,\r\n  `shapers.ts`, and `validators.ts`. That list is exhaustive, a new function kind joins it, and no\r\n  later version of the plugin claims more.\r\n- The plugin reports no `data` violation in `helpers.ts`. The kind rules place a camelCase\r\n  namespace of functions there, and a namespace of callables is not separable from a data table by\r\n  declaration syntax, so `DATA_EXEMPT_FILES` in `configs/policy.ts` excludes the file. Ordinary\r\n  module data there — `export const RETRIES = 3` — is unreported; the earlier constants rule binds\r\n  regardless.\r\n- No placement or line-ending rule inspects an ambient declaration file: `.d.ts`, `.d.mts`, and\r\n  `.d.cts` are outside their reach. The lint population reaches those files, and each of those\r\n  rules refuses the file by its name, because an ambient declaration file is not a module in the\r\n  kind table.\r\n- The plugin does not inspect class-expression members. A function assigned inside a\r\n  class-expression method is unreported; the earlier functions rule still binds, and cleanup and\r\n  review enforce it.\r\n- The cleanup pass and independent review prove kind purity across those files. A helper misfiled\r\n  as a parser, a coercer misfiled as a guard, a compiler misfiled as a factory, and a shaper\r\n  misfiled as a cloner are review findings, not instrument diagnostics.\r\n- The plugin does not decide barrel membership. It parses each file alone and resolves no module,\r\n  so it cannot tell whether a declaration is reachable from its barrel. That question belongs to\r\n  each package's `tests/guides.test.ts`, which imports the barrel and gets real resolution. Do not\r\n  add module resolution here to duplicate it.\r\n- The kind table is mandatory whether or not an instrument can see the violation.\r\n\r\n## Wrapper test\r\n\r\nA wrapper survives only when it adds a real boundary, invariant, composition, translation, lifecycle, or materially narrower contract.\r\n\r\n- Delete one-line delegates, pass-through factories, rename-only helpers/getters, compatibility aliases, and wrappers around semantically identical platform or declared-dependency primitives.\r\n- Rename or import the real symbol and update every consumer atomically. Do not preserve a wrapper to avoid downstream edits.\r\n- Do not re-export a dependency's symbol from this package.\r\n- A public class method composes real entity behavior; it never exists only to forward 1:1 to a helper.\r\n- Audit callers and callees after every extraction so the old path and duplicate implementation do not remain.\r\n\r\n## Functions and orchestration\r\n\r\n- Never declare or assign a function inside another function or method.\r\n- This bans local `function`, `function*`, and `const fn = () => ...`, regardless of caller count.\r\n- The only in-body function expressions allowed are an anonymous callback passed directly as an argument and an anonymous function returned directly as the result (the factory/combinator pattern).\r\n- Instance-bound work that reaches state or sibling methods is a method, not a free function.\r\n\r\nSeparate these roles:\r\n\r\n1. **Public method:** implements the interface and genuinely composes behavior; never forwards 1:1 to one helper.\r\n2. **`#` private method:** stateful/instance-bound orchestration or the class's defining recursive/compositional algorithm.\r\n3. **Centralized helper:** pure referentially transparent leaf, independently understandable/testable without class knowledge.\r\n\r\nApply the leaf test:\r\n\r\n1. Reaches `#` state or a sibling method → private method.\r\n2. Pure self-contained computation (key, format, compare, convert, lookup, projection, one unification) → exported helper.\r\n3. Recursive spine or composition of leaves (`solve`, `isolate`, `prove`, chaining, relational join), even if pure → private method.\r\n4. Defining engine internals remain methods when extraction would hollow the class into a thin delegate.\r\n\r\nExtract pure leaves aggressively; keep the class's actual behavior.\r\n\r\n## Class order\r\n\r\n1. `#` private fields: context, options, state/result, child managers.\r\n2. Constructor: initialize context/options and instantiate child managers.\r\n3. Public interface: getters, then methods.\r\n4. `#` private methods.\r\n\r\nStore child managers in `#` fields and expose readonly getters typed as their interfaces.\r\n\r\n## Middleware\r\n\r\n- Middleware is behavior `(context, next) => ...`, never a class with one `handle` method.\r\n- Place middleware factories in `middlewares.ts` as `createX(options): Middleware`; keep them distinct from entity/value factories.\r\n- Keep middleware-local state closure-private.\r\n- If consumers must address, share, inspect, or replace that state, extract the state to a pluggable class supplied as an option, with an in-memory default.\r\n- Extract reusable cross-middleware machinery to helpers/classes.\r\n- `MiddlewareManager` is the sole manager of the middleware composition chain; do not create one manager per middleware.\r\n\r\n## Environment/module placement\r\n\r\n- Shared cross-environment logic belongs in the central core/shared layer. Other environments import core; core imports neither browser nor server.\r\n- An environment with multiple modules keeps cross-module types/helpers at the environment root and module-specific declarations inside the module.\r\n- Promote a declaration to the environment root only when at least two modules use it.\r\n- The environment barrel re-exports root files and module barrels.\r\n- Cover cross-cutting environment-root helpers with behavioral tests; each module API receives its own guide parity.\r\n\r\n### Entity subfolders\r\n\r\n- When one entity grows a family (entity + manager or sibling implementations), nest only its class files in a lowercase plural folder.\r\n- Keep its interfaces in module-root `types.ts`, factories in module-root `factories.ts`, and exports in the module barrel.\r\n- Entity subfolders never grow their own centralized files.\r\n\r\n### Kind or folder\r\n\r\n- A word is either a centralized kind or a domain folder, never both.\r\n- A folder named for a centralized kind—`helpers/`, `validators/`, `handlers/`—is that kind's file, not a folder.\r\n- `FUNCTION_DOMAIN_FOLDERS` in the fleet-canon register (`configs/policy.ts`) registers the\r\n  folder paths whose direct modules are function modules. Registration makes a path eligible and\r\n  fixes the declaration shape checked there; it judges nothing about what a module does.\r\n- Never infer a function domain from a folder's name; a camelCase module inside an unregistered\r\n  folder is misplaced.\r\n- Register a folder only when its direct modules are camelCase single-function `.ts` modules. A\r\n  folder of PascalCase single-file components, or of entity classes, never qualifies.\r\n- Request a new domain through a fleet-canon change. There is no workspace-local registration path.\r\n\r\n### Extension categories\r\n\r\n- A designed extension point—drivers, stores, transports—gets its category folder even with one concrete class.\r\n- This applies both to consumer implementations of another domain's contract and default implementations beside the defining contract.\r\n- The contract remains in module-root `types.ts`; only concrete classes nest.\r\n- Nest because the category is a designed growth seam, not because a class name happens to end in `Store` or `Driver`.\r\n- Co-equal native-object wrappers that are core primitives stay flat.\r\n\r\n### Stores\r\n\r\nChoose the access shape:\r\n\r\n- **Point access:** `get`, `set`, `delete`.\r\n- **Bulk restore:** `save`, `load`, `remove`, `clear`.\r\n\r\nEvery shape obeys:\r\n\r\n- The stored value carries its own id; do not pass a separate id to `set`/`save`.\r\n- Every primitive is async and returns a `Promise`.\r\n- Deleting/removing a missing key is a no-op.\r\n- Concrete stores live in `stores/`; the interface stays in module-root `types.ts`; `create*Store` factories stay in `factories.ts`.\r\n\r\n## Barrel exports\r\n\r\n- `*/index.ts` is the sole public barrel.\r\n- Only an `index.ts` may re-export.\r\n- A barrel contains only `export * from './module.js'` declarations. Do not use\r\n  named, default, namespace, or type-only barrel exports.\r\n- A star-export collision is a design failure: rename the conflicting concept\r\n  at its owner and update consumers. Never hide the collision with a selective\r\n  barrel row.\r\n- Never re-export a symbol originating in another package; fix consumer imports to the originating package.\r\n- Implementation files export their own classes directly.\r\n- Expose every intentional top-level source export through its correct environment barrel.\r\n- Never let current consumer count gate later exposure. Developers receive the same supported\r\n  mechanisms the package uses, so they retain full control and customization.\r\n- If a declaration must not be public, make it a true local or runtime-private detail, or remove\r\n  the capability for a substantive reason. Never leave an intentional reusable export stranded\r\n  outside the barrel.\r\n- One-class-per-file evicts some classes from their only caller, and `export` on such a file is\r\n  structural rather than a statement of intent. Barrel that class when a consumer can construct it\r\n  from values they already hold. Intern it — out of the barrel, and named in the package's parity\r\n  `INTERNAL` list — when its constructor requires a value only its owner produces, or when the\r\n  public value is a projection of the instance rather than the instance. A class named in a public\r\n  signature is always barrelled.\r\n- Delete a barrel row whose class no consumer can construct, and delete its `@example` with it. A\r\n  row obliges a documented, runnable example, so a class kept public without one is drift that\r\n  parity cannot see.\r\n- When a symbol moves, update every import; never leave a compatibility re-export.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nexport * from './types.js'\r\nexport * from './constants.js'\r\nexport * from './errors.js'\r\nexport * from './validators.js'\r\nexport * from './helpers.js'\r\nexport * from './factories.js'\r\nexport * from './greeters/Greeter.js'\r\n```\r\n\r\n## System constraints\r\n\r\n- Build or substantively expand a capability with its first real consumer; do not speculate. This\r\n  gate applies only when creating or expanding the capability, not to its later barrel exposure.\r\n- Keep interfaces to the smallest primitives the capability requires.\r\n- For multiple backends, implement shared querying/paging/aggregation in one engine over those primitives. A backend may override an operation only with a genuine native fast path and otherwise falls back to the engine.\r\n- Centralize any pattern repeated twice.\r\n- Keep everything generic/reusable and free of unrelated-project logic.\r\n- Do not expand the capability set without concrete need. Once that capability exists intentionally,\r\n  its reusable top-level exports follow the earlier barrel rule without a second consumer gate.\r\n- Do not remove structural files because they are empty.\r\n- Prefer the smallest complete implementation that preserves architecture.\r\n- No deprecation aliases, compatibility shims, or backward-compatibility branches; update all consumers atomically.\r\n- No polling/busy loops or recursive microtasks as architecture. Park idle work on an event/abort wakeup and yield long work in cooperative quanta.\r\n- Framework/server utilities provide mechanism—HMAC, CORS, parsing, sessions, cookies, CSRF, static serving—not product policy such as user models, login flow, or authorization.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - 'src/**/*.ts'\r\n  - 'app/**/*.{ts,vue}'\r\n  - 'tests/**/*.ts'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# API pattern rules\r\n\r\n## Declared ecosystem capabilities\r\n\r\nBefore writing a guard, parser, combinator, outcome, safe exception boundary, contract shape/compiler, schema utility, error narrower, emitter helper, or other general primitive:\r\n\r\n1. Inspect `package.json`, the lockfile, vendored dependency guides, and exact installed declarations/exports.\r\n2. Build a semantic overlap map between the proposed/local behavior and declared `@orkestrel/*` capabilities.\r\n3. Reuse the originating package directly when semantics match.\r\n4. Keep local behavior only when it adds a real domain invariant, composition, projection, translation, or intentionally different contract.\r\n5. Test the difference. Similar names are not proof of equivalent semantics.\r\n\r\nNever reimplement or rename-wrap a declared package primitive. Do not add an ecosystem dependency unless the user explicitly authorizes it.\r\n\r\n## Options\r\n\r\n- Top-level keys are single words.\r\n- Group related settings beneath the configured entity noun; every leaf is one word.\r\n- Never encode grouping through prefixes such as `serverPort` or `databasePath`.\r\n- Reserve `on` exclusively for initial `EmitterHooks`.\r\n- Explain short option keys in TSDoc `@remarks`, not through longer names.\r\n\r\n```ts\r\ninterface ServerOptions {\r\n\treadonly on?: EmitterHooks<ServerEventMap>\r\n\treadonly error?: EmitterErrorHandler\r\n\treadonly server?: { readonly port?: number; readonly timeout?: number }\r\n\treadonly database?: { readonly path?: string }\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\n## Managers\r\n\r\n### Accessors\r\n\r\nManagers expose one item and all items through singular/plural domain nouns:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nentity(key): EntityInterface | undefined\r\nentities(): readonly EntityInterface[]\r\n```\r\n\r\nExamples: `timeout(id)`/`timeouts()`, `agent(id)`/`agents()`.\r\n\r\n### Batch operations\r\n\r\nOne single-word verb carries these overloads:\r\n\r\n```ts\r\nmethod(): void\r\nmethod(id: string): boolean\r\nmethod(ids: readonly string[]): boolean\r\n```\r\n\r\n- No argument applies to all.\r\n- One id applies to one.\r\n- An id list applies to those items and returns true only when all succeed.\r\n- Never split into `methodAll`, `methodOne`, or `methodMany`.\r\n- A manager that owns `clear` and a batch verb keeps both: `clear` resets the entity's state and emits one `clear`, and the no-argument batch verb applies the verb to every item and emits per item. They are different observable operations.\r\n- When a single item type can itself be a list/open record, declare the array overload first and document how callers express one list-valued item.\r\n\r\n## Stateful emitters\r\n\r\nAn entity with lifecycle transitions, constraints, or observable operations owns an emitter by composition:\r\n\r\n1. Define `{Entity}EventMap` in `types.ts`.\r\n2. Add `readonly on?: EmitterHooks<{Entity}EventMap>` and `readonly error?: EmitterErrorHandler` to options.\r\n3. Add `readonly emitter: EmitterInterface<{Entity}EventMap>` to the interface.\r\n4. Store `readonly #emitter: Emitter<{Entity}EventMap>` and expose it through `get emitter()`.\r\n5. Initialize with both hooks and error handling: `new Emitter({ on: options?.on, error: options?.error })`. Positional constructors thread both values equivalently.\r\n6. Emit directly with `this.#emitter.emit(...)`; do not add a guarded `#notify`.\r\n7. Call `this.#emitter.destroy()` last in the entity's `destroy()`.\r\n\r\nNever inherit from `Emitter`, write delegation boilerplate, or use `Omit` to reshape it.\r\n\r\n### Listener isolation\r\n\r\n- The emitter isolates each listener; one throw never prevents sibling listeners.\r\n- It reports every listener throw to its own `(error, event)` error handler.\r\n- It never rethrows a listener error.\r\n- A throwing error handler is swallowed to prevent recursion.\r\n- Never add `observerError` or another listener-failure event to a domain `EventMap`.\r\n- A genuine domain I/O/transport `error` event is distinct and remains valid.\r\n\r\n### Browser/DOM variant\r\n\r\nIn a pure browser-DOM environment where each entity already owns a host `Element`, the element may be the event host:\r\n\r\n- emit a typed bubbling `CustomEvent` through shared dispatch helpers;\r\n- subscribe with shared listener helpers;\r\n- bind `options.on` through one `bindEventMap` helper.\r\n\r\nChoose one event model per environment. `Emitter<TMap>` remains the default outside exclusively DOM-bound environments.\r\n\r\n### Event maps\r\n\r\n- Event names are single present-tense verbs or nouns: `start`, `connect`, `expire`, `exhaust`, `drain`, `chunk`.\r\n- Never publish a generic `status` event carrying a transition value; publish each transition as its own event.\r\n- Type errors as `unknown`.\r\n- Use empty tuples for signals and labeled tuple elements for IDE clarity.\r\n- Keep an entity's event map focused, normally four to eight events.\r\n\r\n## Validation and contracts\r\n\r\nUse these orthogonal contract mechanisms:\r\n\r\n| Surface          | Contract                                                                      |\r\n| ---------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\r\n| `validators.ts`  | Total `is*` guards: `(unknown) => value is T`; no coercion/side effects       |\r\n| `combinators.ts` | Build guards from guards: `arrayOf`, `recordOf`, `unionOf`                    |\r\n| `parsers.ts`     | Flat coercers returning `T \\| undefined`                                      |\r\n| Shape DSL        | One `ContractShape` compiled into schema, guard, parser, and seeded generator |\r\n\r\n- Use plain guards/parsers for small contracts.\r\n- Use the shape DSL only when validation, serialization, and generation parity earns its complexity.\r\n- When `@orkestrel/contract` is declared, use its exact installed guards, parsers, combinators, outcomes, `attempt` boundary, and shape DSL wherever their semantics match. Do not maintain a local copy of those primitives.\r\n- A guard never throws for adversarial input, cycles, deep nesting, or hostile prototypes; return false.\r\n- Recursive guards track ancestors and cap depth.\r\n- Recursion enters only through an explicit lazy gate.\r\n- Non-lazy structural cycles fail at build time, not runtime.\r\n- Parse/guard soundness is mandatory:\r\n  - guard-valid input is never rejected by its parser;\r\n  - every parsed result satisfies its guard.\r\n- Derive parser and guard from one source or test the round trip.\r\n\r\n### Foreign contracts\r\n\r\nA value returned by an interface another package publishes is FOREIGN data. Own it, validate what you dereference, and narrow nothing.\r\n\r\n- Enforce the published contract and no more: accept unknown members, accept any implementation the interface admits including a class instance, and check each member as its published type declares it. A member typed `number` is not checked as an integer.\r\n- Reserve the exact-record guard for a record this package owns. Over a foreign interface it refuses values the interface permits and fails closed on a valid implementation.\r\n- Narrow nothing in an ownership transform. Where the published contract is wider than the copy mechanism, seal the value in place rather than refusing it.\r\n- Own a foreign value at arrival, validate the owned copy, and read the foreign object exactly once. Read count is this package's decision, so no result may depend on it.\r\n- Validate only what the package dereferences from a union it must narrow. Own a wide foreign record it merely carries and leave it unvalidated. State that asymmetry on the option that admits the implementation.\r\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

This section fixes the instruments and how work is assigned\r\n  between them; each rule's substance stays with the law it enforces.\r\n\r\n## Text integrity\r\n\r\n- Store text as UTF-8.\r\n- Before accepting broad generated or migrated edits, scan changed text for replacement characters, mojibake, unintended control characters, and accidental trailing debris.\r\n- Preserve intentional Unicode punctuation and symbols; do not “clean” valid text merely because it is non-ASCII.\r\n- Never renormalize Unicode while rewriting a file. Retyping a line can silently fold a decomposed sequence into its precomposed form — `e` + U+0301 becoming U+00E9 — and the two render identically, so the diff reads as a no-op and review sees nothing. Where the exact code points are the subject, as in an encoding or transport proof, that fold deletes the case the test exists for while leaving it green and named. Move such a line rather than retyping it, and compare bytes with `od -c` or a code-point dump rather than by eye.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - '*.md'\r\n  - 'guides/**/*.md'\r\n  - 'tests/guides.test.ts'\r\n  - 'src/**/types.ts'\r\n  - 'src/**/index.ts'\r\n  - 'app/**/types.ts'\r\n  - 'app/**/index.ts'\r\n  - 'src/styles/**/*'\r\n  - '.agents/skills/**/*.md'\r\n  - '.claude/skills/**/*.md'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Documentation and parity rules\r\n\r\nDocumentation is an enforced contract, not explanatory decoration. The Writing rules in\r\n`AGENTS.md` govern its prose and are not restated here.\r\n\r\n## Authority and workflow\r\n\r\n- Read the matching spec/guide before code, form the intended design, then compare implementation. Existing code is a verification target, not ground truth.\r\n- `AGENTS.md` and its linked rules are the sole convention source. Do not create competing instruction copies in guides.\r\n- `guides/README.md` is the map: maintain both a concept index and a directory index. The concept index runs `spec ↔ source ↔ tests ↔ showcase` minus every column whose subject this workspace lacks, so an app-only workspace that publishes no library and builds no showcase still owes a full index over the columns it has.\r\n- Where the repository keeps one, `ROADMAP.md` is the sequenced plan of record. Each chunk reaches green before the next.\r\n- A showcase is executable proof of public API. A missing demonstration is a missing feature, detectable by parity.\r\n- An integration surface's guide documents the validated hookup for each supported client: the exact commands run, the authentication and approval model that client needs, and the honest limit wherever a client cannot reach part of the surface.\r\n\r\n## Parity\r\n\r\n- Every backticked API in a guide resolves to a real public export.\r\n- Every public export is documented.\r\n- TypeScript, SCSS, Markdown, tests, and showcase remain aligned.\r\n- A parity failure identifies drift; never suppress or weaken the test.\r\n- A guide `Summary` cell equals its export's doc-block description paragraph, both read in the form\r\n  the `findDrift` function compares (a `{@link}` tag written as its target's code token, whitespace\r\n  collapsed, a code span's boundary whitespace trimmed); a titled `@example` equals the guide fence\r\n  under the heading of that title; and the README pitch equals the guide's tagline.\r\n  `tests/guides.test.ts` asserts each through the `findDrift` function and the `tagline` method\r\n  that `@orkestrel/guide` exports. Invoke the public `GuideCommand` class there with the package's\r\n  inventory policy and direct host ports, and register the package assertions in the anonymous\r\n  callback passed to its `execute` method. The command owns argument validation, explicit\r\n  rewriting, and remaining-drift reporting. Run `npm run test:guides` for read-only parity\r\n  assertions. Use\r\n  `npm run test:guides -- --to guide` or `npm run test:guides -- --to source` only when choosing\r\n  an explicit rewrite direction. Before adopting the generated command, implement its direct\r\n  entry and rewrite directions in the package-owned `tests/guides.test.ts`. Scaffold must not\r\n  synthesize or overwrite that authored proof. Update each fleet package's authored file before\r\n  its release. Never weaken the gate.\r\n- The TSDoc voice rule governs a doc block, and a `Summary` cell carries that block's description\r\n  paragraph, so the same voice governs the cell. A guide tagline and a README pitch are noun\r\n  phrases, and each is the blockquote under its file's H1.\r\n- A vendored dependency guide is a mirror. Its relative links address the upstream tree and resolve to nothing here, so they are outside local-link parity. Refresh a mirror rather than rewriting it: a rewritten copy is a translation, and no comparison against the fetched bytes can check it.\r\n- Falsify a prose claim the way you falsify a code claim. The parity test proves a name exists, never that a sentence about behavior is true, so run the example and read what it returns. A `// false` beside a call that returns `true` is a defect of the same kind as a wrong return value, and it reaches every consumer who installs the package. That proof has a home: `tests/guides.test.ts` executes the flagship fences, per `.claude/rules/tests.md`. An ordered behaviour with no gate is not a gate.\r\n  Asserting that the sentence appears is not asserting that it is true. `expect(text).toContain('a spawn fault reports null')` passes unchanged when the code starts returning something else, so it guards the documentation's presence and nothing about the behaviour. Where a prose claim about behaviour sits under no fence, add the executed assertion that would break if the claim went false, and keep the substring check only as a presence guard beside it. A row whose close condition names a behaviour does not close on a substring.\r\n- Re-read the prose last, against what actually shipped. Where a change chose to document a limit rather than close it, the sentence was often drafted for the option that lost, or written more confidently than the code earns. Code rulings survive review because a test can break them; prose rulings survive because nothing tries.\r\n\r\nFor behavioral interfaces/classes:\r\n\r\n- Document public methods under `## Methods`.\r\n- Use one method table per interface, keyed by its backticked name.\r\n- The table's methods exactly match the interface's call-signature members.\r\n- Readonly data properties remain in the interface's `## Surface` row.\r\n- Each implementing class exposes exactly its interface methods—no missing or extra public behavior.\r\n\r\nParity scope:\r\n\r\n- Normally scope a guide to one module directory.\r\n- A layer concept may include the core module and backend modules implementing it.\r\n- Cross-cutting environment-root helpers are covered by behavioral tests rather than forced into one module guide.\r\n\r\n## Guide examples\r\n\r\nCode fences import through the package's published specifier:\r\n\r\n- primary/core API: `@orkestrel/<name>`;\r\n- secondary environment API: `@orkestrel/<name>/<environment>`.\r\n\r\nNever use in-repository `@src/*` aliases in public guide examples; reserve them for source/tests.\r\n\r\n## Workflow skills\r\n\r\n- Skills prescribe reusable process; they do not copy naming, placement, syntax, lifecycle, or test laws from `AGENTS.md` and rules.\r\n- Where a skill works a named subject, name its directory in the `orkestrel-<verb>-<noun>` form with the verb acting on that noun; where a skill runs a process the Orchestrator owns, name it in the `orkestrel-<verb>` form.\r\n- For a portable skill that teaches an external framework, takes the host project's own `AGENTS.md` file as its code law, and binds none of this repository's rule files, drop the `orkestrel-` prefix and name the skill for the framework a reader searches for. Add no other exception. The `enterprise-bootstrap` skill meets that test and keeps its name: it carries Bootstrap 5.3 craft, assumes no stack, and names the `.agents/orchestration.md` and `.claude/rules/quality.md` files only where they are present. Do not rename it into the namespace.\r\n- Keep `SKILL.md` concise and route conditional detail to one-level `references/`.\r\n- Name every Markdown file in a skill's `references/` from its `SKILL.md`, and delete a reference nothing names.\r\n- Frontmatter contains only `name` and a trigger-focused `description`.\r\n- Set `name` to the skill's own directory name.\r\n- Write `description` as a single-line scalar or a folded `>-` block, and no other shape. Include in it a sentence beginning `Use ` that names when to invoke the skill.\r\n- Do not put model routing or package version catalogs in a skill.\r\n- Validate every referenced resource, leave no template TODOs, and limit each skill directory to `SKILL.md`, `agents/openai.yaml`, and the `references/*.md` files its `SKILL.md` names; add no other file or directory.\r\n- Write `agents/openai.yaml` as one root `interface:` mapping over exactly `display_name`, `short_description`, and `default_prompt`, in that order, each on its own two-space-indented line.\r\n- Research the external schema only when a consumer needs a key outside `display_name`, `short_description`, and `default_prompt`, and add no key before then.\r\n- Give every one of those keys a non-empty single-quoted scalar, and write an apostrophe inside it as `''`.\r\n- Name the skill's own `$<directory>` token in `default_prompt`.\r\n- Keep provider bridges minimal: they load one canonical workflow and add no competing instructions.\r\n- Give a provider bridge its canonical skill's `name` and `description` verbatim, name the `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` path it loads, and give it no references of its own.\r\n- Give every canonical skill exactly one provider bridge directory of the same name, and give every bridge directory a canonical twin.\r\n- Review a bridge body's remaining instructions yourself: the policy sweep proves `name` and `description` parity, the named canonical path, and the absence of bridge-owned references, and nothing else about the body.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - 'src/**/*'\r\n  - 'app/**/*'\r\n  - 'configs/**/*'\r\n  - 'tests/**/*'\r\n  - 'scripts/**/*'\r\n  - 'guides/**/*'\r\n  - 'package.json'\r\n  - '.gitattributes'\r\n  - '.github/workflows/*'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Host portability rules\r\n\r\nPublished source runs on Windows and Linux. Write one implementation that is correct on each host,\r\nor gate the host-specific branch on the host that needs it.\r\n\r\nTake the test half from `.claude/rules/tests.md`: it governs a host-varying property inside a test\r\nand the form of a conditional skip.\r\n\r\n## Host branching\r\n\r\n- Branch on a capability a runtime probe answers — a returned value, a thrown code, a present\r\n  member — wherever the difference is detectable.\r\n- Branch on `process.platform` only where the behavior is platform-defined, and name that fact in\r\n  the symbol's `@remarks`.\r\n- Put a platform-literal table — install root, executable suffix, toolchain name — inside a\r\n  `process.platform` branch, and read it on that platform alone.\r\n- Never flatten a correctly-gated host branch into a single path, and never relax a fail-closed\r\n  check to make one host pass.\r\n\r\n## Line endings\r\n\r\n- Split arrived text on `/\\r\\n|\\n/`. Never split on `\\n` alone, and never split on a bare `\\r`.\r\n- Split first, then trim each line; trimming the whole payload leaves a `\\r` on every line but the\r\n  last.\r\n- Frame a named protocol — HTTP, LSP, RFC 6455, NDJSON — with the terminator that protocol fixes,\r\n  and leave that terminator alone.\r\n- Emit `\\n` from code this package owns. Never emit `os.EOL`.\r\n\r\n## Paths\r\n\r\n- Normalize each operand of a path comparison with the same normalizer before comparing.\r\n- Never case-fold a containment check. Fold a lookup key only by the file-name case sensitivity the\r\n  consumer declares, and fold the whole key when you fold at all.\r\n- Compose, split, and relativize a host path with `node:path`. Never concatenate a separator\r\n  literal.\r\n- Keep a storage key, an archive key, and a URI path slash-separated, and refuse `\\` and a drive\r\n  prefix inside one.\r\n- Refuse the Windows reserved device names and the filename characters Windows rejects, on every\r\n  host.\r\n- Read the temporary directory from `os.tmpdir()`. Never write a `/tmp` literal in source.\r\n- Build a `file:` URI with `pathToFileURL`. Never format one from a path string.\r\n- Convert a `file:` URI to a host path with `fileURLToPath`. Never read `URL.pathname` as\r\n  a host path or strip the scheme, decode escapes, or rewrite a drive prefix by hand.\r\n\r\n## Processes and executables\r\n\r\n- Resolve, spawn, and terminate a child through `@orkestrel/process` where the package declares it.\r\n- Where it is not declared, spawn `process.execPath` with a JavaScript entry. Never spawn a `.bin`\r\n  shim, and never add `shell: true` to reach one.\r\n- Take a resolver's first match only after splitting its output into lines and trimming each one.\r\n  `where` prints a match per line and can name a file that is not an executable.\r\n- Treat `fs.constants.X_OK` as an existence check on Windows, where `accessSync` passes on a plain\r\n  file. Confirm an executable by its name and its file type instead.\r\n- Read an `fs.constants` member before putting it in a flag word. Windows leaves `O_NOFOLLOW`\r\n  undefined, so `O_WRONLY | O_NOFOLLOW` refuses nothing there. Never polyfill the missing member:\r\n  compensate with a check the host supports, or record the limit.\r\n- Read `PROGRAMFILES`, `LOCALAPPDATA`, and `APPDATA` inside the win32 branch and `HOME` inside the\r\n  POSIX branch. Never read one host's root on the other.\r\n- Terminate a Windows child by its process tree. A `SIGTERM` handler never runs there, so gate every\r\n  cooperative-shutdown path on POSIX.\r\n- Treat a permission bit as advisory on Windows: a written mode does not round-trip\r\n  (`chmodSync(dir, 0o500)` reads back `0444` there), so assert modes only where a probe reports they\r\n  round-trip.\r\n- Retry an `EBUSY`, `ENOTEMPTY`, or `EPERM` removal inside a bounded budget, and report the failure\r\n  when the budget ends.\r\n\r\n## Terminals\r\n\r\n- Gate styling and interactivity on `isTTY`, `NO_COLOR`, and `FORCE_COLOR`, never on the platform.\r\n- Decode `\\r`, `\\n`, and `\\r\\n` from a key stream as the same key.\r\n- Never call a Windows console-mode API. Node exposes none, so record the unreached behavior as a\r\n  limit.\r\n\r\n## Scripts and packaging\r\n\r\n- Write every `package.json` script as a portable command: a Node invocation or an installed binary.\r\n  Never name a `.sh` file there.\r\n- Keep `#!/usr/bin/env node` on an npm bin. npm writes the Windows shim from it.\r\n- Keep an agent hook under `scripts/` in bash, and keep `.gitattributes` `eol=lf`.\r\n\r\n## Claims\r\n\r\n- Never describe the suite, the host, or CI as POSIX. Name the host and the date behind a reading.\r\n- Run the gate chain on each host a change claims, and record a reading that could not run as a\r\n  limit naming the host it needs.\r\n- Keep a POSIX-shaped `file:///` literal in a guide only as an opaque fictional identifier. Build\r\n  any example a reader executes with `pathToFileURL`.\r\n---\r\npaths:\r\n  - 'src/**/*'\r\n  - 'app/**/*'\r\n  - 'tests/**/*'\r\n  - 'guides/**/*'\r\n  - 'package.json'\r\n  - 'vite.config.ts'\r\n  - 'tsconfig.json'\r\n  - '.agents/skills/**/*'\r\n  - '.claude/skills/**/*'\r\n---\r\n\r\n# Research, hardening, and completion rules\r\n\r\n## Evidence before change\r\n\r\n- Research when the user asks for it, when comparing an upstream, protocol, or legacy implementation, or when current external behavior materially affects design.\r\n- Use current primary sources for external capabilities, and the exact installed declarations and guides for dependencies. Separate verified fact from inference.\r\n- Read authoritative types and named decision-bearing implementation files first-hand. Delegate bulk supporting context, never the owning design decision.\r\n- Treat existing code, tests, `old/`, branches, and copied projects as evidence, not authority.\r\n- Build a capability/defect matrix before a broad API or production-readiness change. Every row ends as implement, repair, retain, or intentionally exclude with evidence.\r\n- Fix that matrix when the change starts. It is the definition of done, and the change closes when every row closes.\r\n- Never end a row as \"hardened further.\" Replace any evaluative phrase with the concrete condition that closes the row.\r\n- Record a finding outside the matrix against the row that owns it, for the next matrix. Do not reopen this one.\r\n\r\n## Probes before arguments\r\n\r\nA question a probe can settle is settled by the probe, whether or not anyone has disputed it. Reasoning decides what to run and what the result means. It does not replace the run.\r\n\r\n- Test your own assumptions before you rely on them. You do not need a disagreement to justify a probe. If you are about to depend on what a function returns, what a config resolves to, what a flag does, or whether a path is even reached, run it and find out.\r\n- Treat a long deliberation about behaviour as the signal to stop and run something. Deliberation that a ten-line probe would have ended is the most expensive habit in this process, and it is invisible because it feels like rigour.\r\n- Verify a belief before stating it, or label it as unverified. An unverified assertion put into context becomes a fact for everything downstream, including other agents, and correcting it later costs more than the check would have.\r\n- Hold a claim you issue to the standard you hold a claim you receive. Nothing downstream re-checks an instruction, so an unverified fact inside one becomes the premise of every task built on it. Auditing every report you receive while exempting every brief you write is the common form of this failure.\r\n- Prefer the check over the chain of reasoning whenever the check is cheap. Reading the installed declaration, running one line, or counting the call sites beats three paragraphs inferring the same thing, and it produces evidence you can hand to someone else.\r\n- Write the probe before the argument gets long. The moment a claim about behaviour is disputed — between engines, between a report and a rule, or in your own head — stop and run it. Get the smallest real input through the real code and read the real output.\r\n- Bound the search before starting it, and put the bound in the brief. Name the benchmark, the population, or the row that ends it. An investigation with no stated stopping condition runs until attention runs out. This includes the case where the honest answer is that the limit is inherent and belongs in documentation rather than in code.\r\n- Treat a negative probe as evidence about the probe until its input is shown to reach the code under test. A pass proves nothing if the vector never arrived. Instrument the path, assert an observable side effect, or drive it through a door you can see.\r\n- When a report names a defect and your reproduction comes back clean, assume first that your vector was weaker than theirs, and go get their exact vector. Treating a failed reproduction as a disproof is the most common way a true finding is lost.\r\n- A reported vector the compiler rejects refutes the VECTOR, never the finding. Where a claim asserts reachability through a typed API, compile the exact vector under the project's own settings; re-derive one the types admit before dropping the finding, and record which vector was actually tested.\r\n- Reproduce a reported defect's cause before instructing a fix from it. A real symptom can carry a wrong diagnosis, and a fix aimed at the stated cause edits the wrong file while the defect survives.\r\n- Do not read a result into a tool's failure to find your probe. \"No tests found\", an empty match, a skipped file, a runner that resolved nothing — these report on the harness, not the subject. Confirm the probe was collected and executed first.\r\n- Prefer an observation over a derivation, including your own. When a measurement and an argument disagree, the argument is wrong until the measurement is shown to be broken.\r\n- Diagnose from the artifact the work produces — the file, the count, the exit code, the timestamp on what changed — never from a wrapper or a proxy signal that merely correlates with progress. Read a gate bare: a pipeline stage after it (`| tail`, `| grep`) reports the stage's exit status and hides the gate's failing lines.\r\n- Verify a comment or an agent's report against the call sites before relying on it. A code comment is not evidence. When lanes disagree about whether a path is live, count the callers rather than weighing the prose.\r\n- What a round proves is what it ran. A conclusion carried from one door to another is a hypothesis at the second door. Re-run it there.\r\n\r\n## Falsification\r\n\r\nA review that reads a diff finds what the diff shows. A review that tries to break named claims finds what the diff hides. Code that has passed diff review several times can still carry a defect nobody has tried to trigger.\r\n\r\n### Writing the claims\r\n\r\n- State the subject as a numbered list of the claims the work makes, never as \"review this diff.\" Each claim is falsifiable: a property some concrete input, state, or interleaving could show false.\r\n- Instruct the auditor to attempt refutation, not confirmation. A claim it cannot break is CONFIRMED with the evidence that convinced it. A claim it breaks is BROKEN with the exact failing input, state, or interleaving, plus the smallest correct fix.\r\n- Derive claims from what the change asserts under adverse conditions: cancellation, restart, concurrency, partial failure, hostile input, resource exhaustion, and the orderings a happy path never reaches.\r\n- Read the installed declaration or implementation of every substrate a claim depends on. A claim about `stop()` is unfalsifiable until you know what `stop()` does when the status is not the one the caller assumed.\r\n\r\n### Instruments\r\n\r\n- An instrument is not evidence until it has failed. Pair every probe, comparison, or matrix with a negative control that must report failure, run under the same conditions. An identity check whose control reports \"same\" has measured nothing.\r\n- When a question about a TypeScript edit can supply a workspace project, a case of workspace files with a test, and a negative control naming its files, its test, the stage it must fail at, and why, call the `prove` tool the `probe` MCP server registers before relying on the answer. When the question supplies no project, no case, or no control, follow `.claude/rules/tests.md` § Probes and report the fallback instrument's own control and coverage.\r\n- When no `probe` server is registered in the session, register one outside the repository, in the harness's own local or user MCP scope — in Claude Code, `claude mcp add` outside project scope — naming the installed `node_modules/@orkestrel/probe/dist/bin/main.js` entry, and start it in the repository whose projects the question names, because the server fixes its workspace from its own working directory. The registration cannot live in the tree: a scaffold target holds no `.mcp.json` file, that path is instruction canon, and a copy at it reports as foreign drift on every `scaffold audit` run. Where the harness registers no server at all, treat the question as supplying no project and take the preceding rule's fallback.\r\n- Quote the closing line of the `prove` answer verbatim in every report, brief, and audit verdict that rests on the claim: the `receipt probe:<digest>:…` line when the case ran clean and the control broke exactly where the claim declared it would, and the `no receipt` line otherwise. A `no receipt` line leaves the claim unproved — report it with the stage that refused.\r\n- Read a receipt as evidence about its claim, never as a gate result. The gate chain still runs, and `verifier` still owns its result.\r\n- Draw the negative control from outside the population the instrument covers. Name the instrument's membership rule first, then pick a control that rule excludes. A control sampled from constructs the instrument already handles proves only that it discriminates among those constructs, and says nothing about the class it silently cannot reach.\r\n- State an instrument's coverage beside its result. A conclusion inherits the instrument's scope, not the question's. An unstated coverage claim is read as complete, and it never is. A search proves something about the paths it walked, so name them.\r\n- Match the instrument to the question. A text search reports on text, so a claim about declarations, call sites, or structure needs the compiler or a parser instead. A pattern written for one spelling of a construct reports on that spelling alone. A path check answers relative to the directory it runs from, so resolve the inputs against their own base before reading a miss as a finding.\r\n- Name the rival reading the instrument must exclude, and show it reports differently under that reading. Give independent measurements independent state: one counter shared across members reports read order and per-member read count identically, so a result consistent with both measured neither.\r\n- Report a question unanswered rather than answering it with a weaker instrument. A fallback that measures something adjacent returns a confident wrong answer, and nothing downstream can tell that answer from the real one — searching commit messages for a release when the question is where a version changed will match some release, but not the one asked about. Name the substitute and what it actually measures, or say the question is open.\r\n- State what the controls established and what they did not. An instrument certified only from the inside is trusted exactly where it has never been tested.\r\n- Treat a gap between what an instrument says it checks and what it actually matches as a defect in the instrument, not as a documented limit. A recorded blind spot buys trust only when everything outside it is genuinely covered.\r\n- Measure the product, not the harness. A recorded baseline that counts something about its own fixture is not evidence about the shipped surface, however often a guide quotes it.\r\n- Baseline a published-artifact claim against the published artifact. \"Did my change move the surface\" and \"does this release differ from the last one\" are different questions, and a diff against your own starting point answers only the first. A toolchain that re-emits declarations moves the artifact without any source edit, so every writer can correctly report an unmoved surface while the package's published contract has changed. Fetch what consumers actually have — the tarball, the deployed asset — and compare against that.\r\n- Prove a module cycle by loading the built artifact, not by a green suite. Tests import through the source graph and a bundler resolves it differently, so a cycle that is fatal at module-init in the shipped form can stay invisible under every test. Import each published entry point and read an export from it.\r\n- Adopt an instrument that settled a claim as a test before accepting the work it settled. The probe that proved a fix, carrying the control that proved the probe, is that fix's regression guard. A verification that runs once is a rehearsal, not a gate.\r\n\r\n### Rounds and verdicts\r\n\r\n- Treat an all-confirmed round as a legitimate result, and put the brief on trial rather than the subject. Re-read the claims and ask whether any could have been falsified by evidence the round actually had. If none could, the claims were descriptive and the round proved nothing — sharpen them and re-run. If they could have been and were not, the pass stands.\r\n- Name the claims you could not break either way, so the next round knows what has already been attacked.\r\n- Never tell an auditor that a clean round means it did not try. That instructs it to manufacture a finding, and a manufactured finding costs a fix unit, an argument, and the credibility of the true findings beside it.\r\n- Treat a repaired claim as a new claim, not a settled one. Re-ask it at every entry point that reaches the same rule, not only the door the defect arrived through. The engine that wrote the fix is least able to see this, because re-verifying where the fix is feels like verifying the fix.\r\n- A fix that adopts the auditor's prescription verbatim may close with a mutation probe in place of a fresh audit round: disable the load-bearing line, watch the adopted pin fail, restore it, and commit the pin as the regression guard. A fix that departs from the prescription gets the cross-engine round.\r\n- Let reachability bound the fix. A defect reachable through the package's own shipped code or a documented extension seam falsifies its claim and is repaired now.\r\n- Document the obligation instead when a defect is reachable only through a hypothetical foreign implementation of a contract this package publishes. State it on the interface that owns it and prove the documentation. Do not build coordination machinery against a requirement nobody wrote down. Attacks are unlimited; reachable ones are not, and only the reachable set is a work list.\r\n- **Three rounds at one seam is the budget, and reaching it switches the search strategy rather than stopping the work.** Repeated rounds against one seam are evidence about the design, not evidence of diligence. At the third round, name what the audit is trying to accomplish, then pick the successor strategy from what the recurrence shows.\r\n- Recurrence with a direction — each fix relocates the class along one stream, or the evidence points up a dependency chain — ends the depth search. Run one breadth round that probes the stream's stations in parallel to locate the source, in the shape `.agents/orchestration.md` § Context and decomposition prescribes, then plan the downstream work from the source and size it by the sweep's map.\r\n- Recurrence with no direction makes the seam itself the question. The next unit is a ruling — on the threat model, the mechanism, or the boundary — taken with the same adversarial pass a design gets, not a fourth repair.\r\n- A subject that reprices itself on every edit — a count, a census, a total over prose — has no closing condition and is not a seam. Drop the claim, or recast it as the property the tally stood in for.\r\n- Write the round count down in the capability/defect matrix row that owns the seam, when the seam opens, so it is a fact rather than a feeling. A seam that has consumed more rounds than the rest of the matrix combined has already answered the question.\r\n- State the ruling that ends a seam as the invariant the code will obey, the constraint bounding it against over-correction, and the interface where a consumer meets the obligation. A ruling that names only the defect it replaces produces the opposite defect next round.\r\n- Give every behavioural audit the means to run its attacks. An auditor that cannot execute cannot falsify a behavioural claim: it returns derivations, and a derivation reads exactly like a verdict while being a different thing — it will confirm a claim one probe would break. Treat a report with no executed evidence as a review of the source, and label it as such.\r\n\r\n## Ecosystem reuse\r\n\r\nThe root laws on inspecting declared `@orkestrel/*` capabilities, reusing a matching primitive, and updating every consumer without shims bind here without restatement. This file adds the judgment calls:\r\n\r\n- Prove the semantic difference before keeping a local variant. Similar names are not evidence of different behavior.\r\n- Treat downstream friction as valid evidence of a reusable upstream defect, not as automatic proof. Fix the lowest package that owns the general mechanism and keep product policy downstream.\r\n- Never re-export a dependency's symbol to soften a consumer's import.\r\n\r\n## Production hardening\r\n\r\n- Translate \"enterprise-grade\" or \"production-ready\" into an explicit risk and seam matrix covering applicable inputs, states, failures, cleanup, cancellation, concurrency, resource ownership, hostile boundaries, environment isolation, serialization and restore, and package consumption.\r\n- Grade that matrix on coverage, not optimization: whether every applicable seam exists, works, and stays proven. Do not grade on how many further interleavings can be invented against the one seam already proven. A surface polished past its row buys less than the next uncovered seam would have.\r\n- Test observable invariants at each applicable seam with real implementations.\r\n- Use dedicated real-service projects for external model or service behavior. Require readiness and tune each request to the smallest proof that still exercises the claim.\r\n- Audit test discovery, counts, skipped and todo tests, cleanup, and assertion adequacy. Passing discovered tests alone is insufficient.\r\n- Inspect public exports, declarations, supported runtime targets, and generated outputs.\r\n- Treat a claim that a surface works with an external client as unproven until one representative real client of that class has driven it end to end. Protocol tests prove the protocol, not the integration.\r\n- Add an independent adversarial review for security, destructive paths, concurrency, protocols, or untrusted external input.\r\n\r\n## Completion\r\n\r\nThe root completion law — finish every in-scope capability now, leave no TODO, deferral, or hidden follow-up, and run the applicable repository skill for comprehensive work — binds here without restatement. This file adds these obligations:\r\n\r\n- Perform a final centralization, wrapper, test-helper, and text-integrity sweep after implementation and before gates.\r\n- Produce local quality gates and relevant output inspection as required evidence.\r\n- Stop once the enumerated scope is closed and those gates are green. Stopping there is correct, and the next scope is the deliverable. A further pass over the same surface requires a new instruction from the user, not an auditor's remaining appetite. Depth is owed to what the scope names, not to what an engine can still imagine about it.\r\n# Developer-facing writing rules\r\n\r\nThese rules govern prose a developer reads, including chat replies, reports, guides, README files,\r\nand commit messages.\r\n`AGENTS.md` § Writing governs prose everywhere, and this file does not restate it; this file adds\r\nonly what a developer audience decides. An instruction file follows `AGENTS.md` § Instruction files\r\nfirst, and these rules wherever a rule here names no different form for it.\r\n\r\n## Voice and actor\r\n\r\n- Write `must` for a requirement, `can` for an option or an ability, and `might` for a possibility.\r\n  A recommendation takes the imperative, the same form `AGENTS.md` § Writing fixes for instructions.\r\n  Never write `should`, and never soften a recommendation into `We recommend`.\r\n- Address the developer as `you`. Name the software component that acts, and make it the subject of\r\n  the sentence. Reserve `user` for someone using software the developer builds. Never write `we`,\r\n  `our`, or `let's` about agent work.\r\n- Give software no human faculties. A component reports, returns, detects, or refuses; it never\r\n  knows, thinks, wants, or sees.\r\n- Use the passive voice only where naming the actor adds blame and nothing else, such as a defect\r\n  count. Name the actor wherever the reader needs it.\r\n- Use negative contractions (`don't`, `isn't`, `can't`) in a reply or a guide. Do not use them in an\r\n  instruction file.\r\n\r\n## Sentence and paragraph order\r\n\r\n- Put the condition, the goal, or the location before the instruction, so a reader the instruction\r\n  does not apply to stops at the first clause. Put the result or the reason after it. This governs a\r\n  sentence, not a document: a report still opens with its finding.\r\n- Put the key point in the first sentence of every paragraph and every list item, not only in the\r\n  opening of the reply or the section.\r\n- Keep the helper words `that`, `then`, `of`, `a`, and `the`. Do not drop one for brevity.\r\n- Name the noun after `this`, `these`, or `it` wherever the reader could attach the pronoun to\r\n  another referent.\r\n- State what the reader can do. Do not write a double negative.\r\n\r\n## Claims and time\r\n\r\n- Claim only what the reader can check. Never write `ensure`, `guarantee`, a superlative, or an\r\n  effort adjective as a claim about behavior, and cite the run behind every number. `ensure`\r\n  addressed to an executor as a directive is outside this ban.\r\n- Write the present tense for what exists. Do not write `currently`, `now`, `new`, `latest`, or\r\n  `soon`; where time matters, give the version or the date.\r\n- Recommend one path, the shortest one you proved. Rule on every option you list, and drop an option\r\n  equivalent to the one you recommend.\r\n\r\n## Code tokens, references, and links\r\n\r\n- Put a code token in backticks and follow it with a noun: the `parse` method, the `vite.config.ts`\r\n  file, the `--check` flag. Never inflect, pluralize, or possessivize a code token, and never use one\r\n  as an English verb.\r\n- Point to other material with `preceding`, `following`, `earlier`, or `later`, never with `above` or\r\n  `below`. Use `earlier` and `later` for a version range too.\r\n- Write link text as the destination's title or a descriptive phrase, introduced by `see`. Never\r\n  write `here`, `this document`, or a bare URL in prose.\r\n- Paraphrase third-party content and link its source; never paste it into prose you author. A\r\n  vendored mirror is fetched bytes rather than authored prose, and `.claude/rules/documentation.md`\r\n  governs it.\r\n\r\n## Structure\r\n\r\n- Keep a required fact in the main flow. A note or a notice carries only what the reader can skip,\r\n  never a prerequisite, a step, or a warning the task depends on.\r\n- In a reply or a guide, introduce every list, table, and code fence with a complete sentence naming\r\n  what follows; a rule file's list sits bare under its heading. Number a list only where order or\r\n  rank matters. Use a table only for rows with comparable fields.\r\n- Write a heading in sentence case, verb first for a task and a noun phrase for a concept. Identity\r\n  numbering — a claim, an audit verdict, a plan unit — is data rather than a heading style, and\r\n  stays.\r\n\r\n## Examples, numbers, and abbreviations\r\n\r\n- Build an example from fictional data with descriptive names and no personally identifiable\r\n  information. Write a placeholder in `UPPER_SNAKE_CASE` and explain it on first use. Never write\r\n  `foo`, `bar`, or `baz`.\r\n- Expand an abbreviation the reader may not know on first use, with the short form in parentheses.\r\n  Skip the expansion for one this audience reads daily, such as `API`, `CLI`, `JSON`, `URL`, or a\r\n  file format.\r\n- Write a numeral for a technical quantity, a version, or a measurement. Write a date as\r\n  `YYYY-MM-DD` in evidence, commit messages, and reports. See `AGENTS.md` § Writing for what\r\n  separates a value from a count.\r\n- Use the serial comma. Mark omitted code with a comment in the sample's language, never with `...`.\r\n\r\n## Substitutions\r\n\r\nReplace each term in this table with its replacement.
